size** | ~25MB | ~3-5GB (PyTorch + models) |\n| **Speed** | Instant | ~1-14s/page (CPU), ~0.2s/page (GPU) |\n\n**Decision**: Use pymupdf unless you need OCR, equations, forms, or complex layout (lightweight)\n\n``​`bash\npip install pymupdf pymupdf4llm\n``​`\n\n**Via helper script**:\n``​`bash\npython scripts/extract_pymupdf.py document.pdf              # Plain text\npython scripts/extract_pymupdf.py document.pdf --markdown    # Markdown\npython scripts/extract_pymupdf.py document.pdf --tables      # Tables\npython scripts/extract_pymupdf.py document.pdf --images out/ # OCR)\n\n``​`bash\n# Check disk space first\npython scripts/extract_marker.py --check\n\npip install marker-pdf\n``​`\n\n**Via helper script**:\n``​`bash\npython scripts/extract_marker.py document.pdf                # Markdown\npython scripts/extract_marker.py document.pdf --json         # JSON with metadata\npython scripts/extract_marker.py document.pdf --output_dir out/  # Save images\npython ```text
I reviewed the PDF text. **Not legal advice**, but here’s a practical business/legal summary with key terms, red flags, and unusual clauses.

## High-level summary

This is an **Amended and Restated Limited Liability Company Agreement** for **Starlight 13, L.L.C.**, a Delaware LLC, dated **May 20, 2026**.

The company appears to be an **investment/SPV vehicle** whose stated purpose is primarily to acquire, own, hold, manage, and dispose of a direct or indirect investment in **Iconiq Strategic Partners VIII Co-Invest, L.P. Series A3**.

The agreement is heavily **Managing Member–controlled**. The Managing Member is **JAWS Ventures, LLC**, and nearly all operational, governance, capital-call, distribution, transfer, amendment, dissolution, and tax decisions sit with the Managing Member, often in its **“sole and absolute discretion.”**

## Key extracted terms

**Company:** Starlight 13, L.L.C.

**Jurisdiction:** Delaware LLC.

**Original formation date:** July 18, 2018.

**Agreement date:** May 20, 2026.

**Managing Member:** JAWS Ventures, LLC.

**Principal office:** 2340 Collins Avenue, Miami Beach, FL 33139, unless changed by Managing Member.

**Purpose:** Limited mainly to holding/managing an investment in **Iconiq Strategic Partners VIII Co-Invest, L.P. Series A3**, plus related/incidental activities, financing, contracts, affiliates, employees/advisors, and lawful incidental business.

**Term:** Perpetual, unless dissolved under the agreement.

**Initial capital contributions:** To be made as shown on **Exhibit A** within **7 calendar days** after the effective date. However, the extracted Exhibit A does not show actual member names, contribution amounts, or percentage interests.

**Additional capital contributions:** Managing Member may call additional capital. Members generally must contribute within **15 business days** after notice.

**Failure to fund capital call:** Member’s percentage interest may be diluted based on relative aggregate capital contributions.

**Distributions:** Available Cash distributions are made at times, amounts, and manner determined by the Managing Member in its sole discretion, generally pro rata by percentage interest.

**Management:** Managing Member has broad exclusive control. Non-managing members have no management authority and cannot bind the company.

**Tax/fiscal year:** Calendar year ending December 31.

**Accounting method:** Cash method unless changed by Managing Member or disallowed.

**Tax representative:** Managing Member is designated as Partnership Representative.

**Reports:** Annual balance sheet, related financial statements, tax return info, and copy of federal income tax return due within **180 days** after fiscal year-end.

**Transfers:** Member transfers are highly restricted. Managing Member written consent required and may be **unreasonably withheld**.

**Substitute members:** Assignee does not become substitute member unless Managing Member consents, transferee adopts agreement, expenses paid, and legal opinion provided.

**Withdrawal:** Member withdrawal/resignation/retirement before expiration is a breach.

**Dissolution:** Managing Member may dissolve the company at any time in its sole and absolute discretion.

**Dispute resolution:** AAA arbitration in the municipality where the company office is located, or as close as possible.

**Governing law:** Delaware law.

**Indemnification:** Broad indemnification of members, organizer, and managers, including advancement of expenses, subject to repayment if not entitled.

## Business takeaways

This is a **manager-controlled investment LLC/SPV agreement**. Passive members appear to be capital providers with limited governance rights. The Managing Member controls:

- Capital calls
- Dilution mechanics
- Distributions and reserves
- Affiliate transactions
- Company debt
- Amendments
- Transfers
- Admission of new members/classes
- Dissolution
- Tax elections and audit positions

If you are a passive investor/member, the biggest practical issue is that your economics and liquidity are largely dependent on the Managing Member’s discretion.

## Red flags / points to review

### 1. Very broad Managing Member discretion

The Managing Member controls management, distributions, reserves, debt, affiliate transactions, sales of assets, and dissolution. Many powers are exercisable in **sole and absolute discretion**.

Why it matters: minority/passive members have very limited ability to influence timing of distributions, reserves, financings, related-party dealings, exits, or liquidation.

### 2. Mandatory capital calls with dilution

Members may be required to make Additional Capital Contributions when the Managing Member determines they are necessary. Failure or refusal causes dilution.

Why it matters: this creates future funding exposure. If a member cannot or does not fund a capital call, ownership can be diluted. You’d want to know whether there is any cap on capital calls, budget approval right, notice detail, or purpose limitation.

### 3. Distributions fully discretionary

Available Cash distributions are made only as determined by the Managing Member. The Managing Member may also suspend, reduce, or restrict distributions of Available Cash and Liquidation Proceeds.

Why it matters: even if the company has cash, members may not have a clear right to receive it on a fixed schedule.

### 4. No fiduciary duties

Section 6.9 expressly waives fiduciary duties except contractual duties expressly provided.

Why it matters: this is significant. The Managing Member/member may consider only the interests/factors it desires, including its own interests, and may act even when market conditions are materially disadvantageous to other members.

### 5. Affiliate transactions permitted

The Managing Member may cause the company to enter into transactions or agreements with affiliates in its sole and absolute discretion.

Why it matters: related-party transactions can create conflicts. There does not

Required whenever the\n# server's folder names don't match himalaya's canonical names\n# (inbox/sent/drafts/trash). Gmail is the common case — see\n# `references/configuration.md` for the `[Gmail]/Sent Mail` mapping.\nfolder.aliases.inbox = \"INBOX\"\nfolder.aliases.sent = \"Sent\"\nfolder.aliases.drafts = \"Drafts\"\nfolder.aliases.trash = \"Trash\"\n``​`\n\n> **Heads up on the alias syntax.** Pre-v1.2.0 docs used a\n> `[accounts.NAME.folder.alias]` sub-section (singular `alias`).\n> v1.2.0 silently ignores that form — TOML parses fine, but the\n> alias resolver never reads it, so every lookup falls through to\n> the canonical name. - OAuth client secret missing: `/opt/data/google_client_secret.json`

## Important: “all three G Drives”

There are two possible meanings:

1. **One Google account can already see all three drives** — personal, 166 2nd, and Flow as My Drive / Shared Drives / shared folders.  
   → We only need to authorize **that one Google account** with full Workspace scopes.

2. **They are three separate Google accounts**.  
   → We need to authorize each account separately. Hermes’ default Google setup stores one active token, so I’ll set up a clean multi-account token structure after the first auth works.

## Next step

Please upload or send me the path to your **Google OAuth client JSON** — the file downloaded from Google Cloud Console, usually named something like:

`client_secret_....apps.googleusercontent.com.json`

If you don’t have it handy, create/download it here:

1. Go to: https://console.cloud.google.com/apis/credentials
2. Select the same project you used before, or create one.
3. Make sure these APIs are enabled:
   - Gmail API
   - Google Calendar API
   - Google Drive API
   - Google Docs API
   - Google Sheets API
   - People API
4. Credentials → Create Credentials → **OAuth client ID**
5. Application type: **Desktop app**

[DJ Mauch] is this the secure way to do this?
```

### assistant

```text
Mostly yes — **OAuth is the secure way**, but with a few important caveats.

## Secure parts

Using Google OAuth means:

- You do **not** give me your Google password.
- Google shows you the exact permissions being requested.
- You can revoke access anytime from your Google Account.
- Hermes receives a revocable token, not your login credentials.
- We can scope access to Gmail / Calendar / Drive / Docs / Sheets instead of account-wide password access.

This is much better than sharing a Gmail password or app password.

## The sensitive part

The OAuth setup uses two files:

1. **OAuth client JSON**  
   This identifies the Google Cloud OAuth app. It is not your Gmail password, but it should still be treated as sensitive-ish.

2. **Google token file**  
   This is the truly sensitive one. It allows Hermes to access Google services you approved. It must stay private and stored only on this Hermes machine at:

`/opt/data/google_token.json`

I will not ask you to paste the token into chat.

## Best-practice secure setup

The safest version is:

1. You create the OAuth client in your own Google Cloud project.
2. You upload only the **Desktop OAuth client JSON** here.
3. I generate a Google consent URL.
4. You open that URL directly with Google.
5. You approve only the scopes you want.
6. You paste back the final redirect URL/code.
7. Hermes stores the token locally.
8. You revoke access anytime here:  
   https://myaccount.google.com/permissions

## Bigger security question: “full access”

You asked for full access to Gmail + Calendar + Drive + Docs + Sheets across personal, 166 2nd, and Flow. That is powerful. It means Hermes could, technically, read and modify a lot of data.

Operationally, I’ll still follow safety rules:

- I won’t send emails without approval.
- I won’t delete Drive files without approval.
- I won’t share files without approval.
- I won’t modify Docs/Sheets without approval.
- I’ll read/search only what you ask me to read/search.

But from Google’s perspective, the token will have broad access.

## My recommendation

Use OAuth, but create a dedicated Google Cloud OAuth client named something like:

`Hermes Agent DJ Workspace Access`

Then authorize only the Google accounts/drives you actually want Hermes to access.

If you want maximum security, we can also do this in phases:

1. **Read-only Drive + Gmail first**
2. Confirm it works
3. Add write/send scopes later only if needed

So: **yes, OAuth is the right secure approach**, but “full access” is broad. If you’re comfortable with that, upload the OAuth client JSON and I’ll proceed.
```

Hermes works with any LLM provider (OpenRouter, Anthropic, OpenAI, DeepSeek, local models, and 15+ others) and runs on Linux, macOS, and WSL.\n\nWhat makes Hermes different:\n\n- **Self-improving through skills** — Hermes learns from experience by saving reusable procedures as skills. When it solves a complex problem, discovers a workflow, or gets corrected, it can persist that knowledge as a skill document that loads into future sessions. Skills accumulate over time, making the agent better at your specific tasks and environment.\n- **Persistent memory across sessions** — remembers who you are, your preferences, environment details, and lessons learned. Pluggable memory backends (built-in, Honcho, Mem0, and more) let you choose how memory works.\n- **Multi-platform gateway** — the same agent runs on Telegram, Discord, Slack, WhatsApp, Signal, Matrix, Email, and 10+ other platforms with full tool access, not just chat.\n- **Provider-agnostic** — swap models and providers mid-workflow without changing anything else. Credential pools rotate across multiple API keys automatically.\n- **Profiles** — run multiple independent Hermes instances with isolated configs, sessions, skills, and memory.\n- **Extensible** — - `/opt/data/google_client_secret.json`

## Secure way to proceed

Best approach:

1. Put the Google OAuth client JSON into Railway as a secret/env var, or upload it temporarily through a secure channel.
2. In the Railway service shell/runtime, materialize it to:

4. You open the consent URL and authorize.
5. Paste back the redirected URL/code.
6. Exchange it **in Railway**:

   python3 /opt/data/skills/productivity/google-workspace/scripts/setup.py --check-live

### assistant

```text
You’re right: we need to authorize **three separate Google identities** and store each token separately in **Railway Chief’s persistent `/opt/data`**, not this local/session box.

Below is the step-by-step.

## Goal

Create three separate Google OAuth token stores:

``​`bash
/opt/data/google-accounts/personal/google_token.json
/opt/data/google-accounts/166-2nd/google_token.json
/opt/data/google-accounts/flow/google_token.json
``​`

Each account will have full requested scopes:

- Gmail read / send / modify
- Calendar
- Drive
- Docs
- Sheets
- Contacts read-only

## Step 0 — Do this in Railway Chief, not locally

Open the **Railway service shell** for the production Hermes/Chief service.

Use either:

- Railway dashboard → your Hermes/Chief service → **Shell**
- or Railway CLI SSH/shell into the running service, if you use that

Confirm you’re in the right runtime:

``​`bash
echo "$HERMES_HOME"
pwd
ls -la /opt/data
``​`

You want `HERMES_HOME` to effectively be `/opt/data`, or at minimum you want `/opt/data` mounted and writable.

## Step 1 — Confirm Google Workspace skill exists

Run:

``​`bash
ls -la /opt/data/skills/productivity/google-workspace/scripts/
``​`

You should see:

``​`bash
setup.py
google_api.py
``​`

If not, check whether the skill path is under the Hermes install instead:

``​`bash
find /opt -path '*google-workspace/scripts/setup.py' 2>/dev/null
``​`

For the commands below, I’ll assume:

``​`bash
GSETUP=/opt/data/skills/productivity/google-workspace/scripts/setup.py
GAPI=/opt/data/skills/productivity/google-workspace/scripts/google_api.py
``​`

Set those:

``​`bash
export GSETUP=/opt/data/skills/productivity/google-workspace/scripts/setup.py
export GAPI=/opt/data/skills/productivity/google-workspace/scripts/google_api.py
``​`

## Step 2 — Create or reuse one Google OAuth client

You can use the **same OAuth client JSON** for all three Google accounts.

In Google Cloud Console:

1. Go to:  
   https://console.cloud.google.com/apis/credentials

2. Pick or create the project for Hermes.

3. Enable these APIs:  
   https://console.cloud.google.com/apis/library

   Enable:

   - Gmail API
   - Google Calendar API
   - Google Drive API
   - Google Docs API
   - Google Sheets API
   - People API

4. Go to Credentials → Create Credentials → **OAuth client ID**

5. Application type: **Desktop app**

6. Name it something like:

   `Hermes Chief Railway Google Access`

7. Download the JSON file.

8. If the app is in **Testing**, add all three Google accounts as test users here:  
   https://console.cloud.google.com/auth/audience

Add:

- your personal Google account
- the `166 2nd` Google account
- the `Flow` Google account

## Step 3 — Put OAuth client JSON into Railway securely

Do **not** paste the token into chat.

You need the OAuth client JSON file inside Railway. Recommended path:

``​`bash
/opt/data/google_client_secret.json
``​`

Options:

### Option A — Railway secret/env var

If you store the JSON contents in a Railway variable like `GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET_JSON`, then inside Railway shell run:

``​`bash
printf '%s' "$GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET_JSON" > /opt/data/google_client_secret.json
chmod 600 /opt/data/google_client_secret.json
``​`

### Option B — Upload/copy file into Railway shell

If Railway shell supports file upload/copy, place it at:

``​`bash
/opt/data/google_client_secret.json
``​`

Then:

``​`bash
chmod 600 /opt/data/google_client_secret.json
``​`

Verify it is valid JSON without printing secrets:

``​`bash
python3 - <<'PY'
import json
p='/opt/data/google_client_secret.json'
data=json.load(open(p))
print('OK client secret JSON. top-level keys:', list(data.keys()))
PY
``​`

Expected top-level key is usually:

``​`bash
installed
``​`

## Step 4 — Create separate account token directories

Run:

``​`bash
mkdir -p /opt/data/google-accounts/personal
mkdir -p /opt/data/google-accounts/166-2nd
mkdir -p /opt/data/google-accounts/flow

chmod 700 /opt/data/google-accounts
chmod 700 /opt/data/google-accounts/personal
chmod 700 /opt/data/google-accounts/166-2nd
chmod 700 /opt/data/google-accounts/flow
``​`

## Step 5 — Install Google Python deps in Railway if needed

Run:

``​`bash
python3 "$GSETUP" --install-deps
``​`

If that fails because pip is unavailable, use apt only if the Railway image supports it:

``​`bash
apt-get update
apt-get install -y python3-googleapi python3-google-auth-oauthlib python3-google-auth-httplib2
``​`

Then check:

``​`bash
python3 - <<'PY'
for m in ['googleapiclient','google_auth_oauthlib','google.oauth2.credentials']:
    try:
        __import__(m)
        print(m, 'OK')
    except Exception as e:
        print(m, 'MISSING', e)
PY
``​`

## Step 6 — Authorize account 1: personal

Run:

``​`bash
export HERMES_HOME=/opt/data/google-accounts/personal

python3 "$GSETUP" --client-secret /opt/data/google_client_secret.json
python3 "$GSETUP" --auth-url
``​`

It will print a long Google OAuth URL.

Open that URL in your browser.

Important:

- Sign in with your **personal** Google account.
- Approve all requested scopes.
- The browser will likely fail redirecting to `http://localhost:1`. That is expected.
- Copy the **entire final redirected URL** from the browser address bar. It will look like:

``​`text
http://localhost:1/?state=...&code=...&scope=...
``​`

Back in Railway shell, exchange it:

``​`bash
export HERMES_HOME=/opt/data/google-accounts/personal

python3 "$GSETUP" --auth-code 'PASTE_FULL_REDIRECT_URL_HERE'
python3 "$GSETUP" --check-live
``​`

Expected success:

``​`text
AUTHENTICATED...
LIVE_CHECK_OK...
``​`

## Step 7 — Authorize account 2: 166 2nd

Run:

``​`bash
export HERMES_HOME=/opt/data/google-accounts/166-2nd

python3 "$GSETUP" --client-secret /opt/data/google_client_secret.json
python3 "$GSETUP" --auth-url
``​`

Open the URL.

This time sign in with the **166 2nd** Google account.

Copy the entire `http://localhost:1/?code=...` redirect URL.

Then:

``​`bash
export HERMES_HOME=/opt/data/google-accounts/166-2nd

python3 "$GSETUP" --auth-code 'PASTE_FULL_REDIRECT_URL_HERE'
python

Here’s exactly how to get the Google OAuth JSON file.

## Get the Google OAuth client JSON

1. Open Google Cloud Console:  
   https://console.cloud.google.com/

   - **Google Sheets API**
   - **People API**

Yep — I set the core security/privacy settings in the **Railway Chief config** at:

`/opt/data/config.yaml`

Current security posture is now:

- **Secret redaction:** enabled  
  `security.redact_secrets: true`

- **Private URL access:** disabled  
  `security.allow_private_urls: false`

- **PII redaction for gateway messages:** enabled  
  `privacy.redact_pii: true`

- **Shell/command approvals:** manual  
  `approvals.mode: manual`

- **Cron approval mode:** deny by default  
  `approvals.cron_mode: deny`

- **MCP reload confirmation:** enabled  
  `approvals.mcp_reload_confirm: true`

- **Destructive slash command confirmation:** enabled  
  `approvals.destructive_slash_confirm: true`

I also verified the active config file is valid.

One note: `tirith_enabled` is currently `true`, but the `tirith` binary is **not installed/found** in the Railway runtime, so Hermes is configured to fail open on that check:

`security.tirith_fail_open: true`

I left that as-is rather than flipping it to fail-closed and risking command/tool failures. The important practical protections — secret redaction, PII redaction, private URL blocking, and manual approvals — are now set.

These config changes generally require a **gateway restart** to fully apply to new Telegram messages.
```

```

### assistant

```text
Recommended strict Google-account security posture:

## Proposed Google permissions policy

### 1. Absolute rule: no sending on your behalf

Hermes should **never send email or messages from any Google account**, including:

- `personal` → `[REDACTED_EMAIL]`
- `166-2nd` → `[REDACTED_EMAIL]`
- `flow` → `[REDACTED_EMAIL]`

This includes:

- no new Gmail messages
- no replies
- no forwards
- no calendar invites that email guests
- no Drive share notifications
- no Docs/Sheets comments that notify people

If you ever want something sent, Hermes may **draft the exact text only**, and you must manually send it yourself outside Hermes.

### 2. Read/search only by default

Default allowed actions:

- Search Gmail
- Read Gmail messages
- Summarize Gmail threads
- Search Drive
- Read Drive file metadata
- Read Docs/Sheets content when requested
- Read Calendar events
- Summarize Calendar availability/events
- Read Contacts metadata only when needed for disambiguation

Default denied actions:

- Send Gmail
- Reply to Gmail
- Modify Gmail labels/read status/archive/delete
- Create/edit/delete Calendar events
- Create/edit/delete Docs
- Create/edit/delete Sheets
- Upload/download/delete/move Drive files
- Share Drive files
- Change permissions
- Create comments/suggestions
- Modify contacts

### 3. Account-specific handling

Hermes must always identify which account it is using before accessing Google data.

Allowed account labels:

- `personal`
- `166-2nd`
- `flow`

If your request is ambiguous, Hermes should ask or choose the least-sensitive likely account only for read-only search. For anything sensitive, it must ask.

Example acceptable behavior:

> “I’m going to search Flow Drive for ‘Q4 budget’ read-only.”

Example unacceptable behavior:

> Searching all three accounts silently without saying which ones were accessed.

### 4. Cross-account search rule

Searching all three accounts is allowed only when you explicitly ask for broad search, e.g.:

- “Search all my Google accounts for…”
- “Check personal, 166 2nd, and Flow…”
- “Look everywhere for…”

Otherwise Hermes should search only the account you named or the most contextually obvious account.

### 5. No destructive or mutating Google actions

Hermes should not perform destructive or mutating Google actions unless we later create a separate, explicit exception process.

Blocked actions include:

- delete email
- archive email
- mark email read/unread
- change labels
- delete Drive files
- move Drive files
- rename Drive files
- edit Docs/Sheets
- create calendar events
- delete calendar events
- update calendar events
- share files
- revoke file permissions
- upload files
- create folders

### 6. Draft-only workflow for outbound communications

If you ask Hermes to “respond,” “send,” “email,” “invite,” or “share,” Hermes should interpret that as:

> Draft the proposed content and ask DJ to handle the actual send/share manually.

Hermes can provide:

- subject line
- body
- recipients to consider
- attachments/links to include
- calendar invite details

But it should not execute the send/share/create action.

### 7. Sensitive content handling

Hermes should treat all Google content as sensitive by default.

Rules:

- Do not quote full emails unless needed.
- Prefer summaries over raw dumps.
- Avoid exposing personal data unless directly relevant.
- Do not include OAuth tokens, message IDs, file IDs, or private URLs unless needed for the task.
- Do not persist email/document contents to memory.
- Do not add Gmail/Drive contents to skills.
- Do not include Google content in cron jobs unless explicitly approved.

### 8. Cron / recurring monitors

No recurring Google monitors unless you explicitly approve:

- account(s)
- search scope
- frequency
- output destination
- cost/token expectations
- whether summaries may include sensitive details

Default: **no Google cron jobs.**

### 9. Tooling enforcement recommendation

In addition to behavioral rules, I recommend we enforce this technically:

- Use wrappers that expose only read-only Google operations by default.
- Block `gmail send`, `gmail reply`, `gmail modify`.
- Block Calendar create/delete.
- Block Drive upload/share/delete.
- Block Docs/Sheets create/update/append.
- Keep the full OAuth scopes only because Google auth is already configured, but restrict Hermes’ practical use through local wrapper policy.

### 10. Emergency revocation

If anything looks wrong, revoke access here:

https://myaccount.google.com/permissions

And remove tokens from Railway:

- `/opt/data/google-accounts/personal/google_token.json`
- `/opt/data/google-accounts/166-2nd/google_token.json`
- `/opt/data/google-accounts/flow/google_token.json`

## Recommended concise policy to save

I’d save this as the durable rule:

> DJ has three Google account slots in Railway Chief: personal, 166-2nd, and flow. Hermes may read/search/summarize Gmail, Drive, Calendar, Docs, Sheets, and Contacts only when requested. Hermes must never send email, reply, forward, create calendar invites, share Drive files, notify people, or otherwise act externally on DJ’s behalf. Outbound communication/share/calendar actions are draft-only. Google write/delete/modify actions are denied by default unless DJ explicitly changes this policy later. Hermes should identify which Google account it is accessing and avoid persisting Google content to memory or skills.

Review that, and if you approve, I’ll save it and then implement a read-only wrapper posture so the tooling matches the policy.
```

{"path":"/opt/data/google-accounts/SECURITY_POLICY.md","content":"# Google Account Security Policy — Railway Chief\n\nApplies to Google account slots:\n\n- `personal` → `[REDACTED_EMAIL]`\n- `166-2nd` → `[REDACTED_EMAIL]`\n- `flow` → `[REDACTED_EMAIL]`\n\n## Absolute outbound communication rule\n\nHermes must never send email, replies, or forwards from DJ's Google accounts.\n\nBlocked:\n\n- Gmail `send`\n- Gmail `reply`\n- Gmail forwarding or any equivalent outbound Gmail operation\n\nIf DJ asks Hermes to send/respond/email someone, Hermes may draft the content only. DJ sends it manually outside Hermes unless this policy is explicitly changed later.\n\n## Notification rule\n\nHermes must not trigger external notifications from Google products.\n\nBlocked or draft-only unless DJ explicitly changes this policy later:\n\n- Calendar invites that email guests\n- Drive share notifications\n- Docs/Sheets comments or suggestions that notify people\n\n## Allowed Google actions\n\nHermes may perform these actions when DJ requests them and the account is identified or contextually clear:\n\n- Search/read/summarize Gmail\n- Search/read/download Drive files\n- Upload/move/delete Drive files\n- Share Drive files and change permissions, but without notification emails\n- Read/create/edit/delete Calendar events, but not guest-invite emails\n- Read/create/edit/delete Docs\n- Read/create/edit/delete Sheets\n- Create comments/suggestions only when they do not notify people\n- Read Contacts metadata when needed for disambiguation\n\n## Account routing\n\nHermes should use explicit account labels:\n\n- `personal`\n- `166-2nd`\n- `flow`\n\nHermes should identify which account it is accessing for sensitive operations. Cross-account search is allowed when DJ asks to search broadly, e.g. “search all Google accounts.”\n\n## Data handling\n\n- Treat Google data as sensitive by default.\n- Prefer summaries over raw dumps unless raw content is needed.\n- Do not persist email/document contents to memory or skills.\n- Do not include Google content in recurring jobs unless DJ explicitly approves the account, scope, frequency, and destination.\n\n## Technical enforcement\n\nThe `/opt/data/scripts/google-account` wrapper blocks known outbound-notification actions:\n\n- `gmail send`\n- `gmail reply`\n- `drive share --notify`\n- `calendar create --attendees ...`\n\nThe underlying token scopes may allow more; policy and wrapper restrictions govern Hermes usage.\n"}

```text
Yes — I’m starting to understand the system.

Here’s what I see from the doc:

## How the doc works

It’s a **daily rolling top-of-mind / todo log**.

### Structure

At the very top:

- `DM Running Daily Top of Mind`
- `[Next date]`
- Latest dated block, currently: **May 20, 2026**

Each dated block is grouped into sections like:

- `[Professional]`
- `[Professional - MENA]`
- `[Professional - Others]`
- `[Personal]`

Older daily snapshots continue below, reverse chronological.

### Tasks

Each task is generally a line item.

Examples:

- `Head of operations - JD and moc ** [n:3fc67]`
- `MENA: Mgmt deal structure template *** [n:d4fe4]`
- `Revamp workout program [n:c7e66]`

### Persistent task IDs

Most tasks have an ID like:

`[n:3fc67]`

My read: these are stable task identifiers that persist across days, so the same task can be tracked over time even if the wording changes slightly.

### Completion

Completed tasks are marked with:

`✅`

Example:

`✅ Software review [n:c0822]`

So a task can appear active on one day, then completed on a later daily snapshot.

### Priority / emphasis

Stars seem to indicate priority or importance:

- `**` = important
- `***` = very important / top priority

Current high-priority examples:

- `Head of operations - JD and moc **`
- `MENA: Mgmt deal structure template ***`
- `MENA: Close F&B contract ***`
- `MENA: Renewal upsell discussions **`

### Carry-forward behavior

The doc appears to work like this:

1. You create a new daily block near the top.
2. You carry forward still-open tasks from the prior day.
3. You add new tasks.
4. You mark completed tasks with ✅.
5. Completed tasks remain visible in that dated snapshot but generally do not carry forward into the next active list.
6. Older blocks become history/audit trail.

### Current active snapshot

The current May 20 block has roughly:

- Professional: 4 active items
- Professional - MENA: 13 active items
- Professional - Others: 1 active item
- Personal: 5 active items

No ✅ items in the current May 20 block, which makes sense if it’s the current working list.

## What I saved

I saved the core convention to memory:

> This Google Doc is your daily rolling todo log; latest block near top; grouped by Professional / MENA / Others / Personal; active tasks repeat with `[n:id]`; ✅ marks completion; `**` / `***` indicate priority.

## How I should use it going forward

I should be able to help with:

- “What are my current top priorities?”
- “What changed since the last update?”
- “Which MENA items are stale?”
- “What got completed between May 13 and May 18?”
- “Draft today’s new block from yesterday’s open items”
- “Clean up duplicates”
- “Turn this into a daily briefing”
- “Find long-running items that keep carrying forward”
- “Suggest what should be removed, escalated, or scheduled”

I won’t edit it unless you ask me to.
```

No Google Cloud project needed.\n  Load the himalaya skill and follow its setup instructions.\n\n- **Email + Calendar** → Continue with this skill, but use\n  `--services email,calendar` during auth so the consent screen only asks for\n  the scopes they actually need.\n\n- **Calendar/Drive/Sheets/Docs only** → Continue with this skill and use a\n  narrower `--services` set like `calendar,drive,sheets,docs`.\n\n- **Full Workspace access** → Continue with this skill and use the default\n  `all` service set.\n\n**Question 2: \"Does your Google account use Advanced Protection (hardware\nsecurity keys required to sign in)? {"success": true, "name": "hermes-agent", "description": "Configure, extend, or contribute to Hermes Agent.", "tags": ["hermes", "setup", "configuration", "multi-agent", "spawning", "cli", "gateway", "development"], "related_skills": ["claude-code", "codex", "opencode"], "content": "---\nname: hermes-agent\ndescription: \"Configure, extend, or contribute to Hermes Agent.\"\nversion: 2.1.0\nauthor: Hermes Agent + Teknium\nlicense: MIT\nplatforms: [linux, macos, windows]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [hermes, setup, configuration, multi-agent, spawning, cli, gateway, development]\n    homepage: https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent\n    related_skills: [claude-code, codex, opencode]\n---\n\n# Hermes Agent\n\nHermes Agent is an open-source AI agent framework by Nous Research that runs in your terminal, messaging platforms, and IDEs. It belongs to the same category as Claude Code (Anthropic), Codex (OpenAI), and OpenClaw — autonomous coding and task-execution agents that use tool calling to interact with your system. Hermes works with any LLM provider (OpenRouter, Anthropic, OpenAI, DeepSeek, local models, and 15+ others) and runs on Linux, macOS, and WSL.\n\nWhat makes Hermes different:\n\n- **Self-improving through skills** — Hermes learns from experience by saving reusable procedures as skills. When it solves a complex problem, discovers a workflow, or gets corrected, it can persist that knowledge as a skill document that loads into future sessions. Skills accumulate over time, making the agent better at your specific tasks and environment.\n- **Persistent memory across sessions** — remembers who you are, your preferences, environment details, and lessons learned. Pluggable memory backends (built-in, Honcho, Mem0, and more) let you choose how memory works.\n- **Multi-platform gateway** — the same agent runs on Telegram, Discord, Slack, WhatsApp, Signal, Matrix, Email, and 10+ other platforms with full tool access, not just chat.\n- **Provider-agnostic** — swap models and providers mid-workflow without changing anything else. Credential pools rotate across multiple API keys automatically.\n- **Profiles** — run multiple independent Hermes instances with isolated configs, sessions, skills, and memory.\n- **Extensible** — plugins, MCP servers, custom tools, webhook triggers, cron scheduling, and the full Python ecosystem.\n\nPeople use Hermes for software development, research, system administration, data analysis, content creation, home automation, and anything else that benefits from an AI agent with persistent context and full system access.\n\n**This skill helps you work with Hermes Agent effectively** — setting it up, configuring features, spawning additional agent instances, troubleshooting issues, finding the right commands and settings, and understanding how the system works when you need to extend or contribute to it.\n\n**Docs:** https://hermes-agent.nousresearch.com/docs/\n\n## Quick Start\n\n``​`bash\n# Install\ncurl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/main/scripts/install.sh | bash\n\n# Interactive chat (default)\nhermes\n\n# Single query\nhermes chat -q \"What is the capital of France?\"\n\n# Setup wizard\nhermes setup\n\n# Change model/provider\nhermes model\n\n# Check health\nhermes doctor\n``​`\n\n---\n\n## CLI Reference\n\n### Global Flags\n\n``​`\nhermes [flags] [command]\n\n  --version, -V             Show version\n  --resume, -r SESSION      Resume session by ID or title\n  --continue, -c [NAME]     Resume by name, or most recent session\n  --worktree, -w            Isolated git worktree mode (parallel agents)\n  --skills, -s SKILL        Preload skills (comma-separate or repeat)\n  --profile, -p NAME        Use a named profile\n  --yolo                    Skip dangerous command approval\n  --pass-session-id         Include session ID in system prompt\n``​`\n\nNo subcommand defaults to `chat`.\n\n### Chat\n\n``​`\nhermes chat [flags]\n  -q, --query TEXT          Single query, non-interactive\n  -m, --model MODEL         Model (e.g. anthropic/claude-sonnet-4)\n  -t, --toolsets LIST       Comma-separated toolsets\n  --provider PROVIDER       Force provider (openrouter, anthropic, nous, etc.)\n  -v, --verbose             Verbose output\n  -Q, --quiet               Suppress banner, spinner, tool previews\n  --checkpoints             Enable filesystem checkpoints (/rollback)\n  --source TAG              Session source tag (default: cli)\n``​`\n\n### Configuration\n\n``​`\nhermes setup [section]      Interactive wizard (model|terminal|gateway|tools|agent)\nhermes model                Interactive model/provider picker\nhermes config               View current config\nhermes config edit          Open config.yaml in $EDITOR\nhermes config set KEY VAL   Set a config value\nhermes config path          Print config.yaml path\nhermes config env-path      Print .env path\nhermes config check         Check for missing/outdated config\nhermes config migrate       Update config with new options\nhermes login [--provider P] OAuth login (nous, openai-codex)\nhermes logout               Clear stored auth\nhermes doctor [--fix]       Check dependencies and config\nhermes status [--all]       Show component status\n``​`\n\n### Tools & Skills\n\n``​`\nhermes tools                Interactive tool enable/disable (curses UI)\nhermes tools list           Show all tools and status\nhermes tools enable NAME    Enable a toolset\nhermes tools disable NAME   Disable a toolset\n\nhermes skills list          List installed skills\nhermes skills search QUERY  Search the skills hub\nhermes skills install ID    Install a skill (ID can be a hub identifier OR a direct https://…/SKILL.md URL; pass --name to override when frontmatter has no name)\nhermes skills inspect ID    Preview without installing\nhermes skills config        Enable/disable skills per platform\nhermes skills check         Check for updates\nhermes skills update        Update outdated skills\nhermes skills unins

Otherwise fall back to MCP search+fetch.\n    14|4. **Parallelism: up to 6 Notion MCP calls in parallel.** The \"max 3\" rule from the old version was too conservative.\n    15|5. **Batch writes into single Docs `batchUpdate` calls.** Multiple `insertText` + `replaceAllText` operations in one HTTP request.\n    16|\n    17|## CRITICAL RULES (task semantics)\n    18|1. **`[>]`/`>`/`↗️` markers are NEVER carried into a new date section.** Strip on carry-over.\n    19|2. **`[x]`/`x`/`X`/`✅` tasks are NEVER carried over.** They're done.\n    20|3. **Also support legacy emoji markers:** ✅ = `[x]`, ↗️ = `[>]`.\n    21|4. **Verify today's date** with `date` command before creating sections.\n    22|5. **Use the local ID map** at `tasks/notion_id_map.json` to look up full page IDs from `[n:xxxxx]` tags. Update the map with any newly created IDs.\n    23|6. **Parking `[Xd]` marker:** `[3d]`/`[5d]`/`[7d]` etc. = \"park for X days.\" On carry-over, move to `[Parking Lot]` section with marker converted to absolute `[M/DD]` (today + X days). Parked items with return date <= today get pulled into today's section (marker stripped).\n    24|7. **Priority `*`/`**`/`***` markers:** carry as-is. {"content": "     1|# CLAUDE.md\n     2|\n     3|## Tone and Communication\n     4|\n     5|- Be direct and concise. One or two sentences, not a lecture.\n     8|- If DJ says go, go. Don't over-confirm.\n     9|- **DJ is non-technical.** Do not assume knowledge of architecture, tradeoffs, or implementation details. When a decision point involves technical tradeoffs, explain the options in plain language with a clear recommendation and why.\n    10|\n    11|---\n    12|\n    13|## How I Work\n    14|\n    15|### Core Standards\n    16|- **Simplicity First**: Make every change as simple as possible. Minimal code impact.\n    17|- **No Laziness**: Find root causes. No temporary fixes. Senior developer standards.\n    18|- **Minimal Impact**: Changes should only touch what's necessary. Avoid introducing bugs.\n    19|- **Verify Before Done**: Never mark a task complete without proving it works. Ask: \"Would a staff engineer approve this?\"\n    20|\n    21|### Planning Discipline\n    22|- Enter plan mode for ANY non-trivial task (3+ steps or architectural decisions)\n    23|- If something goes sideways, STOP and re-plan immediately — don't keep pushing\n    24|- Use plan mode for verification steps, not just building\n    25|- Write the plan to `tasks/todo.md` with checkable items before executing\n    26|\n    27|### Task Tracking\n    28|1. Write plan to `tasks/todo.md` with checkable items\n    29|2. Check in with DJ before starting implementation on complex tasks\n    30|3. Mark items complete as you go\n    31|4. Summarize changes at each step\n    32|5. Add review section to `tasks/todo.md` when done\n    33|6. Update `tasks/lessons.md` after any corrections\n    34|\n    35|### Self-Improvement\n    36|- After ANY correction from DJ, update `tasks/lessons.md` with the pattern\n    37|- Write rules that prevent the same mistake from recurring\n    38|- Review `tasks/lessons.md` at session start\n    39|\n    40|### Verification\n    41|- Never mark a task complete without proving it works\n    42|- Diff behavior between before and after when relevant\n    43|- Run tests, check logs, demonstrate correctness\n    44|- If a fix feels hacky: stop, step back, implement the elegant solution\n    45|\n    46|---\n    47|\n    48|## How I Coach DJ\n    49|\n    50|Don't just execute — help DJ get better results by proactively suggesting better approaches.\n    51|\n    52|### When DJ Gives a Complex Task as a Single Prompt\n    53|Suggest breaking it into subagent work:\n    54|> \"This has a few independent pieces. Want me to use subagents — one for [X], one for [Y], and one to review the output? That'll be faster and higher quality than doing it sequentially.\"\n    55|\n    56|### When DJ Gives Step-by-Step Instructions for Something Complex\n    57|Suggest plan mode:\n    58|> \"Before I start building, let me draft a plan in tasks/todo.md so we can align on the approach. That way I can likely one-shot the implementation.\"\n    59|\n    60|### When DJ Asks for Something Vague or Underspecified\n    61|Don't guess. Ask for specs:\n    62|> \"I want to get this right. Can you give me a bit more on [specific gap]? The more precise the requirements, the better my first attempt will be.\"\n    63|\n    64|### When a Task Involves Multiple Data Sources or Systems\n    65|Suggest subagent delegation:\n    66|> \"I can pull this together faster with subagents — one working on [source A], one on [source B], then I'll synthesize. Want me to go that route?\"\n    67|\n    68|### When Something Goes Wrong Mid-Task\n    69|Don't keep pushing. Say so:\n    70|> \"This isn't going as planned. Let me re-plan rather than keep patching. Here's what I think went wrong: [X].\"\n    71|\n    72|### When DJ Accepts a Mediocre Output\n    73|Challenge it:\n    74|> \"This works, but I think there's a cleaner approach. Want me to take another pass?\"\n    75|\n    76|### When a Session is Getting Long and Context-Heavy\n    77|Suggest subagent offloading or session reset:\n    78|> \"My context is getting heavy.
